Sustain evergreen value through ongoing management.
Performance Monitoring: Search ranking for target keywords. Organic traffic trends. Backlink acquisition rate. Engagement metrics (time on page, return visits, bookmarking).
Update Execution: Accuracy corrections as information changes. Expansion when new subtopics emerge. Quality improvements based on competitive changes. Technical optimization as search algorithms evolve.
Promotion Refresh: Periodic promotion to new audiences and influencers. Social sharing of updated versions. Email notification to subscribers of significant updates. Link building campaigns to new relevant sites.
Decay Detection: Traffic decline indicating relevance loss or competitive displacement. Ranking drops for target keywords. Reduced engagement suggesting content fatigue. Trigger for major revision or replacement.

4. Failure Modes
Four failure modes occur when evergreen content is poorly executed:
Failure Mode One: The False Evergreen
Topic assumed to be evergreen but actually trend-dependent or time-bound. Content that decays rapidly despite evergreen intention. Investment wasted on content with limited persistence. Search volume and relevance decline within months of publication.
Prevention: Search demand analysis over 2+ year period. Evaluation of topic stability. Avoidance of tactics, tools, and trends with limited lifespan. Focus on principles, fundamentals, and enduring problems.
Failure Mode Two: The Outdated Authority
Evergreen content created but not maintained. Information becomes inaccurate; links break; examples stale. Content that was authoritative becomes unreliable. Search ranking declines; audience trust erodes; referral value diminishes.
Prevention: Maintenance schedule and resource allocation. Update triggers based on performance monitoring. Version control for change tracking. Accuracy review as standard operational procedure.
Failure Mode Three: The Comprehensive Bore
Evergreen content that achieves comprehensiveness through padding and repetition rather than substance. Length without depth. Audience abandons before finding value. Search ranking may achieve traffic, but engagement and conversion fail.
Prevention: Substance requirements for every section. Editing for density and value. User testing for engagement sustainment. Quality standards that prohibit comprehensiveness through filler.
Failure Mode Four: The Link Neglect
Evergreen content created without promotion strategy. Quality content fails to earn links because audience never discovers it. Search ranking limited by weak backlink profile. Compounding returns never materialize because visibility remains low.
Prevention: Promotion budget and plan for initial link acquisition. Outreach to influencers and relevant sites. Social and email promotion to existing audience. Ongoing link building as standard practice.

5. Concept Lock-In
Evergreen content retains relevance and value over extended periods through strategic topic selection and structural design. It serves persistent discovery, reference value, and link accumulation functions.
Evergreen is engineered persistence, not accidental longevity. Topic identification analyzes search demand persistence, competitive landscape, and business alignment. Content production emphasizes fundamental principles, comprehensive scope, and quality investment. Maintenance sustains value through performance monitoring, update execution, and promotion refresh.
Failure modes include false evergreen (trend-dependent topics), outdated authority (maintenance neglect), comprehensive bore (padding without substance), and link neglect (promotion failure).
Your content strategy practice must treat evergreen content as foundational investment requiring substantial resources and ongoing maintenance. It provides compounding returns that timely content cannot match and forms the persistent base of sustainable audience acquisition.

Workbook Exercises — Chapter 5.5
Exercise 1: Evergreen Topic Identification
Select a business and identify evergreen topics using Activity One. Analyze search demand persistence, competitive landscape, and business alignment. Prioritize topics by persistence and value. Produce Evergreen Topic Portfolio.
Exercise 2: Evergreen Content Production
Develop one evergreen content piece using Activity Two. Engineer for persistence through topic selection, structural design, and quality investment. Plan update schedule and maintenance procedures.
Exercise 3: Maintenance System Design
Design evergreen maintenance system using Activity Three. Specify performance monitoring metrics, update triggers, promotion refresh procedures, and decay detection protocols. Calculate maintenance resource requirements.
Exercise 4: Failure Mode Prevention
Evaluate existing or planned evergreen content against four failure modes. Identify risks of false evergreen, outdated authority, comprehensive bore, or link neglect. Design prevention measures and quality gates.
 5.6 Timely / Trend-Based Content

Here are questionnaires tailored to each service (content strategy, videography, and business photography) to help you gather the necessary details from potential clients. These questions will help you understand their goals and needs before starting work with them.

Questionnaire for Content Strategy Audit (Posts 1-5)
What are your current marketing goals? (e.g., increase brand awareness, drive more traffic, generate leads)
What digital marketing channels are you currently using? (e.g., social media, email marketing, website, paid ads)
What type of content are you currently creating and sharing? (e.g., blogs, videos, social media posts, infographics)
What challenges are you facing with your current content strategy? (e.g., low engagement, not enough reach, unclear messaging)
Who is your target audience? (Please describe in terms of demographics, interests, etc.)
How do you measure the success of your content efforts? (e.g., web traffic, social media engagement, sales conversions)
What makes your business stand out from competitors?
What’s your budget for content marketing services?
What content goals would you like to achieve over the next 6 months to a year?
Have you worked with a content strategist before? If so, what worked well or didn’t?

Questionnaire for Videography and Video Marketing (Posts 6-10)
What type of video content are you interested in creating? (e.g., promotional videos, product demos, customer testimonials, explainer videos)
What’s the primary goal of your video project? (e.g., brand awareness, lead generation, product launch, education)
Who is your target audience for this video content?
Do you have any existing video content? If so, what kind and how has it performed?
What’s the key message or story you want to communicate through the video?
Where will the video content be used? (e.g., social media, website, email campaigns, paid ads)
Do you have a preferred video style or format? (e.g., animated, live-action, documentary-style, cinematic)
Do you have a timeline or deadline for this project?
What’s your budget for video production and marketing?
Are there any specific competitors or brands whose video content you admire?

Questionnaire for Business Photography Services (Posts 11-15)
What type of business photography do you need? (e.g., product shots, headshots, event coverage, office or location photos)
What’s the purpose of the photos? (e.g., website, social media, marketing materials, product catalog)
Who is your target audience for the photography? (e.g., potential clients, investors, social media followers)
Do you have a specific style or look in mind for the photos? (e.g., clean and modern, bold and colorful, warm and inviting)
Where will the photos be used? (e.g., website, print ads, social media, e-commerce platform)
Do you have a brand style guide or color palette that should be followed in the photography?
Are there any particular products, people, or locations you need photographed?
What’s the timeline for when you need these photos?

Here’s a comprehensive Content Strategy Guide tailored for small to medium-sized business owners. This guide is divided into clear, actionable sections that offer valuable insights while positioning Carr Multimedia as an expert resource.

Content Strategy Guide for Small Business Owners
How to Plan, Create, and Distribute Content that Drives Business Growth

Introduction: Why Content Strategy Matters for Small Businesses
Content marketing isn’t just for big brands. As a small or medium-sized business, you can use content to grow your audience, build trust, and drive more traffic to your business. But random acts of content creation aren’t enough—you need a strategy.
A content strategy is a plan for creating and distributing valuable, relevant content to attract and engage your target audience. This guide will walk you through the steps of developing a successful content strategy that aligns with your business goals.

Step 1: Define Your Goals
Before you create any content, it’s crucial to know why you’re creating it. Your goals will guide every aspect of your content strategy, from what topics to cover to how you’ll measure success.
Key Questions to Ask:
What do I want my content to achieve? (e.g., increase brand awareness, generate leads, educate customers)
What actions do I want my audience to take after engaging with my content? (e.g., signing up for a newsletter, scheduling a consultation, making a purchase)
How does content fit into my overall business goals? (e.g., growing revenue, expanding market share)
Common Content Goals:
Brand Awareness: Increase visibility and make your brand recognizable.
Lead Generation: Attract potential customers through valuable content.
Customer Engagement: Build a community around your brand through ongoing interaction.
Sales Enablement: Use content to educate your audience and move them down the sales funnel.
Customer Loyalty & Retention: Nurture existing customers with helpful content that adds value.

Step 2: Understand Your Audience
Knowing your audience is the cornerstone of effective content marketing. If you don’t know who you’re speaking to, your content will miss the mark. Start by creating buyer personas—fictional representations of your ideal customers based on real data.
Key Questions to Ask:
Who is my ideal customer? (e.g., business owners, marketing managers)
What are their pain points and challenges?
What types of content do they prefer to consume? (e.g., blogs, videos, infographics)
Where do they spend their time online? (e.g., Facebook, Instagram, LinkedIn)
Buyer Persona Template:
Demographics: Age, gender, location, job title, income level
Goals: What do they hope to achieve in their business or personal life?
Challenges: What obstacles or pain points are they trying to overcome?
Preferred Content: What type of content are they more likely to engage with?
Where They Engage: Which platforms do they use most?

Step 3: Perform a Content Audit
If you’ve already been creating content, it’s time to take stock of what you have. A content audit helps you evaluate the performance of your existing content and identify gaps in your strategy.
How to Perform a Content Audit:
List all your existing content: Blog posts, social media updates, videos, eBooks, etc.
Analyze performance: Use tools like Google Analytics, Facebook Insights, or Instagram Analytics to see what content has performed well.
Identify gaps: Are there key topics your audience cares about that you haven’t covered yet?
Repurpose content: Can any of your existing content be updated or repurposed into a different format (e.g., turning a blog post into a video)?

Step 4: Create a Content Plan
With your goals and audience in mind, it’s time to create a content plan. This is a roadmap that outlines what content you’ll create, when you’ll create it, and where it will be published.
Key Elements of a Content Plan:
Content Types: Decide what formats will work best for your audience. Common types include:
Blog posts
Videos
Infographics
Case studies
Podcasts
Social media posts
Topics and Themes: Choose topics that address your audience’s pain points and align with your business goals. Use keyword research tools (like Google Keyword Planner or Ubersuggest) to discover relevant search terms.
Content Calendar: Plan your content over the next 3 to 6 months. Make sure to include a variety of content types and themes, and schedule them based on your audience’s preferences and behavior.
Distribution Channels: Decide where to publish your content. Common channels include:
Social media (Facebook, Instagram, LinkedIn)
Your website or blog
Email newsletters
Video platforms (YouTube, Vimeo)

Step 5: Create High-Quality Content
Great content is valuable, relevant, and consistent. Aim to create content that is helpful, solves problems, and engages your audience.
Key Tips for Creating Content:
Value First: Always create content that solves a problem or adds value to your audience’s life or business.
Be Consistent: Whether you're posting weekly or bi-weekly, set a schedule and stick to it.
Brand Voice: Maintain a consistent voice and tone that reflects your brand’s personality.
Visuals Matter: Use high-quality images, infographics, or videos to make your content more engaging.
SEO Best Practices: Use relevant keywords, optimize meta descriptions, and structure your content for search engines.

Step 6: Distribute Your Content
Creating great content is only half the battle. You also need to make sure it reaches your target audience. Choose the right distribution channels based on where your audience spends their time and how they like to consume content.
Common Distribution Channels:
Social Media: Share content across your social platforms, but tailor your approach to each one (e.g., more visuals for Instagram, articles for LinkedIn).
Email Marketing: Build an email list and share your content directly with your subscribers.
Paid Ads: Promote your content through Facebook Ads, Google Ads, or sponsored posts on social media.
Partnerships: Collaborate with influencers, partners, or local businesses to expand your reach.
Content Syndication: Republish your content on platforms like Medium or LinkedIn to reach a broader audience.

Step 7: Measure and Optimize Your Strategy
Content marketing is a long-term game, and success doesn’t happen overnight. Regularly measure the performance of your content and optimize your strategy based on the data.
Key Metrics to Track:
Traffic: How many people are visiting your website or content pages?
Engagement: How many likes, shares, and comments is your content receiving?
Conversions: Are visitors taking the desired actions (e.g., signing up, purchasing)?
SEO Performance: Is your content ranking for the right keywords and driving organic traffic?
Tools for Measuring Success:
Google Analytics: For website traffic and behavior insights.
Social Media Analytics: Facebook Insights, Instagram Analytics, LinkedIn Analytics for platform-specific metrics.
Email Marketing Tools: Track open rates, click-through rates, and conversions from email campaigns.

Conclusion: Putting It All Together
A successful content strategy doesn’t have to be complicated, but it does need to be intentional. By understanding your goals, audience, and the types of content that will resonate most with them, you’ll be able to create a plan that helps your business grow.
If you’re ready to take your content strategy to the next level, Carr Multimedia is here to help. We offer free content strategy audits and can work with you to develop a custom plan that fits your business needs.

Here's a comprehensive Video Marketing Tips guide that you can use as a lead magnet to attract potential clients for your videography and video marketing services. This guide offers valuable tips while showcasing Carr Multimedia as an expert in the field of video marketing.

10 Video Marketing Tips to Boost Your Business in 2024
Proven Strategies for Small and Medium-Sized Businesses

Introduction: Why Video Marketing is Essential for Your Business
Video marketing is no longer a “nice-to-have” for businesses—it's a necessity. Whether you’re promoting a product, showcasing a service, or engaging with your audience, video is one of the most powerful tools at your disposal. In fact, studies show that 86% of businesses use video as a marketing tool, and it’s proven to generate higher engagement, build trust, and convert viewers into customers.
But creating effective video content requires more than just pressing record. This guide will walk you through 10 actionable video marketing tips that can help you attract more customers, grow your brand, and drive business results.

Tip 1: Know Your Audience
Before you even think about creating a video, you need to know who your audience is. Understanding their needs, preferences, and pain points will guide every aspect of your video marketing strategy.
How to Get Started:
Create Buyer Personas: These fictional profiles represent your ideal customers. Include details like demographics, interests, and challenges.
Analyze Your Existing Audience: Use social media insights, website analytics, and customer surveys to learn more about who is engaging with your content.
Tailor Your Messaging: Once you know your audience, craft your video’s message to address their specific needs and pain points.

Tip 2: Use Storytelling to Engage Your Viewers
People love stories, and storytelling is one of the most powerful ways to capture attention and build an emotional connection with your audience. A well-told story can make your brand more relatable and memorable.
Key Elements of a Great Story:
Characters: Every story needs a relatable character, whether it’s your customer, your team, or even your product.
Conflict: Highlight a problem or challenge that your audience can relate to.
Resolution: Show how your product or service solves that problem and improves their lives.
Example:
Instead of just listing the features of a new product, create a story that shows how a customer used it to solve a real problem. This makes the video more engaging and memorable.

Tip 3: Focus on the First 10 Seconds
The first 10 seconds of your video are critical. Studies show that 20% of viewers will click away from a video within 10 seconds if they aren’t engaged. You need to grab their attention right away.
How to Hook Your Audience:
Start with a Bold Statement or Question: Capture interest with a strong opening line that speaks to your audience’s needs.
Use Eye-Catching Visuals: Show something visually interesting in the first few seconds.
Keep it Short: Avoid lengthy intros—get to the point quickly to keep viewers engaged.

Tip 4: Create Different Types of Videos for Different Goals
There isn’t a one-size-fits-all approach to video marketing. Different types of videos serve different purposes in your marketing funnel. To maximize your results, create a mix of videos that align with your business goals.
Types of Videos to Consider:
Brand Awareness Videos: These tell the story of your brand and introduce your business to new audiences.
Product Demos or Explainer Videos: Use these to show off how your product or service works.
Customer Testimonials: Build trust by letting your satisfied customers tell their success stories.
Educational or How-To Videos: Share valuable knowledge that positions your brand as an expert in your field.
Behind-the-Scenes Videos: Give your audience a peek behind the curtain to build a personal connection.

Tip 5: Optimize Your Videos for SEO
Videos can drive traffic to your website, but only if people can find them. Optimizing your videos for search engines is crucial to getting your content discovered.
Key SEO Optimization Tips:
Use Relevant Keywords: Include target keywords in your video titles, descriptions, and tags to help search engines understand what your video is about.
Write Detailed Video Descriptions: Include a well-written description that explains the content of your video and links to your website.
Use Engaging Thumbnails: Thumbnails are the first thing people see when your video appears in search results. Make sure they are eye-catching and relevant to the content.
Enable Closed Captions: Search engines can crawl closed captions, helping your video rank for keywords used in the dialogue.

Tip 6: Include a Clear Call-to-Action (CTA)
Every video should have a clear call-to-action (CTA) that tells viewers what you want them to do next. Whether it’s subscribing to your channel, visiting your website, or contacting you for more information, make sure your CTA is straightforward and compelling.
How to Craft a Strong CTA:
Be Specific: Instead of just saying “Visit our website,” say “Visit our website today to schedule your free consultation.”
Make It Visible: Include the CTA in the video itself (e.g., text overlay) and in the video description.
Create a Sense of Urgency: Use phrases like “limited time” or “act now” to encourage viewers to take immediate action.

Tip 7: Repurpose Your Video Content
Don’t let your video content live in one place. Repurpose it across different channels to get more value out of each video. This also helps you reach different segments of your audience where they are most active.
Ways to Repurpose Videos:
Social Media Clips: Trim down longer videos into bite-sized clips for platforms like Instagram, Facebook, and TikTok.
Blog Posts: Turn the video script or transcript into a blog post or an article.
Email Campaigns: Embed your video in an email to increase engagement.
Infographics or Slides: Use key points from your video to create visuals for LinkedIn or your website.

Tip 8: Leverage Video Ads to Target Specific Audiences
Paid video ads can be a highly effective way to reach your target audience, especially on platforms like Facebook, YouTube, and Instagram. These platforms offer advanced targeting options to get your video in front of the right people.
Best Practices for Video Ads:
Target Specific Demographics: Use the ad platform’s targeting tools to define your audience based on location, interests, and behavior.
Keep Ads Short: Aim for 15-30 seconds for video ads. Capture attention quickly and get your message across efficiently.
Test Different Versions: Experiment with different video formats, CTAs, and targeting options to see what works best.

Tip 9: Measure Performance and Adjust Your Strategy
To ensure your video marketing efforts are effective, you need to track performance and adjust your strategy accordingly. This allows you to understand what’s working and what needs improvement.
Key Metrics to Track:
Views: How many people are watching your video?
Engagement: Are people liking, sharing, and commenting on your video?
Watch Time: How long are viewers watching your video? Are they staying until the end?
Click-Through Rate (CTR): How many people are clicking on your CTA or visiting your website after watching the video?
Tools to Measure Success:
YouTube Analytics: Get insights into how your videos are performing on YouTube.
Facebook and Instagram Insights: Track engagement and reach on social platforms.
Google Analytics: Measure traffic from videos embedded on your website.

Tip 10: Collaborate with Professionals
While DIY videos can be effective, high-quality professional videos can take your marketing to the next level. Partnering with a professional videographer or video marketing agency ensures your content looks polished and delivers your message clearly.
Benefits of Professional Video Production:
High Production Value: Ensure your videos look professional, with great lighting, sound, and editing.
Creative Direction: Professionals can help you tell a compelling story that aligns with your brand.
Efficiency: Save time and effort by working with experts who can manage the entire process from scriptwriting to post-production.

Conclusion: Take Your Video Marketing to the Next Level
Video marketing is one of the most powerful ways to grow your business in 2024. By following these tips, you can create high-quality video content that engages your audience, builds trust, and drives results. But remember, video marketing is a long-term strategy. Consistency and continuous optimization are key to success.

The Ultimate Business Photography Checklist
This lead magnet can serve as a helpful guide for your clients while subtly promoting your business photography services. Here’s the checklist in an interactive format and step-by-step instructions to make it interactive on your WordPress site using Divi.

The Ultimate Business Photography Checklist
1. Pre-Session Planning:
Identify Key Goals:
☐ What are the primary objectives of this shoot? (e.g., headshots, product photos, team photos)
☐ Who is the target audience for the photos?
☐ Where will these images be used? (e.g., website, social media, print ads)
Select a Location:
☐ On-site (your business office or store)
☐ Outdoor (for a more natural look)
☐ Studio (for a professional, controlled environment)
Confirm Shoot Date and Time:
☐ Confirm the availability of your photographer.
☐ Make sure the location is prepared and accessible.

2. Preparing for the Shoot:
Organize Subjects:
☐ Who will be photographed? (individual team members, groups, products)
☐ Ensure everyone knows the time and place.
☐ Plan any necessary wardrobe or dress code (formal, casual, brand colors).
Prepare the Environment:
☐ Clean and declutter the space if shooting in your office or store.
☐ Ensure proper lighting or notify the photographer of lighting needs.
Brand Representation:
☐ Ensure any logos, branded elements, or signage are prepared.
☐ Align the background and setting with your company’s brand identity.

3. During the Shoot:
Get Involved:
☐ Offer direction if you have a specific vision, but also allow the photographer creative freedom.
☐ Check camera angles and composition to ensure alignment with brand tone.
Engage Team Members:
☐ Encourage team members to smile and relax for natural-looking images.
☐ Capture candid moments to showcase team dynamics.
Product Photography:
☐ Ensure products are clean and properly styled for their best presentation.
☐ Discuss angles, close-ups, or specific features with your photographer.

4. Post-Session:
Review & Edit:
☐ Ask for initial proofs to review the best images.
☐ Provide feedback to the photographer regarding preferred edits.
Deliverables:
☐ Confirm file formats and sizes (e.g., high-resolution for print, web-optimized for website).
☐ Organize files for easy access (team photos, headshots, product images).
Using the Images:
☐ Ensure the images are uploaded to your website, social media, and marketing materials.
☐ Track engagement and performance of new visuals across platforms.

5. Bonus Tips:
Backup Your Files:
☐ Store all images in cloud storage or a dedicated hard drive for future use.
Re-shoot as Needed:
☐ Plan to update your business photos every year or when significant changes occur (e.g., new team members, new products).

Instructions to Make the Checklist Interactive on WordPress Using Divi:
Step-by-Step Instructions:
Create a New Page or Post on Your WordPress Website:
Go to your WordPress dashboard.
Click on Pages or Posts and then select Add New.
Activate the Divi Builder:
Once you’ve opened the new page or post, click on the Use Divi Builder button at the top of the page.
Choose Build from Scratch to create a customized layout for your interactive checklist.
Add a Title for the Checklist:
Add a Text Module and enter your title: "The Ultimate Business Photography Checklist."
Customize the font, size, and style according to your brand’s aesthetics.
Create the Interactive Checklist:
Below the title, add another Text Module for each section of your checklist.
You’ll now make the checklist interactive by adding checkboxes that users can click to track their progress.
Add Interactive Checkboxes Using the Divi Toggle Module:
For each checklist item (e.g., “Identify Key Goals”), use a Toggle Module or Accordion Module.
Go to the Divi Module Library, search for Toggle, and add it to your page. You can create multiple toggles for each point in the checklist.
Title each toggle with your checklist item, and add detailed information inside the toggle content area.
For simple checkbox interactivity:
Inside the toggle module's content, manually add HTML code for the checkboxes.
html
Copy code
<label><input type="checkbox"> Identify Key Goals</label><br>
<label><input type="checkbox"> Select a Location</label><br>
<label><input type="checkbox"> Confirm Shoot Date and Time</label>


Customize the Design:
Go into the Design tab of each Toggle Module and adjust settings like fonts, colors, background, and spacing to ensure they fit your branding.
Customize the look of the checkboxes using custom CSS if needed:
In the Advanced tab, enter custom CSS under the “Main Element” section:
css
Copy code
input[type="checkbox"] {
    width: 20px;
    height: 20px;
}


Create Multiple Sections for the Checklist:
Repeat steps 4-6 for each section of your checklist (Pre-Session, Preparing, During, Post-Session, and Bonus Tips).
Use Dividers or Section Breaks to separate the sections for easy navigation.
Make the Checklist Saveable or Printable:
To allow users to print the checklist, add a Print Button. You can add a Button Module and link it to a JavaScript print function:
In the Button Module’s Link URL, paste the following:
javascript
Copy code
javascript:window.print();


Preview and Test:
After completing the design, preview the page to ensure everything looks and works as intended.
Test the interactivity of the checkboxes and toggles to make sure they function properly.
Publish the Page:
Once you’re happy with the layout and interactivity, hit Publish.

Additional Tips for Enhancing the User Experience:
Add a Progress Bar: You can add a progress bar to show users how far they’ve come in completing the checklist. Divi allows you to easily add a Progress Bar Module to any part of your page.
Include a Downloadable Version: Offer a PDF download of the checklist for users who prefer to print and use a physical copy. Add a link to the PDF under the checklist or within a button module.
Add Email Capture: If you’re using the checklist as a lead magnet, include an Email Opt-In Module at the top or bottom of the checklist page to encourage users to subscribe before accessing the full checklist.

By following these steps, you can create a visually appealing, fully interactive checklist using Divi. This "Ultimate Business Photography Checklist" will not only be a valuable resource for your visitors but also help you gather leads and establish Carr Multimedia’s authority in business photography.

Prioritize key elements like your CTA by positioning them in the first viewport (above the fold) and using larger, bolder fonts or brighter colors to draw attention.
Add section dividers (e.g., thin lines, waves, or gradients) to create a smooth flow between different sections of content.
8. Interactive Features:
Incorporate hover animations on buttons or images to add a layer of interactivity and engagement.
Add a scroll-triggered animation that gradually reveals text or images as the user scrolls.
These changes can enhance the user experience and make the site visually appealing, leading to more engagement and conversions.

ChatGPT
Here are step-by-step instructions to make the changes you want on WordPress using Divi:
1. Layout Changes
Hero Section:
Step 1: Go to the page editor in WordPress and click “Enable Divi Builder.”
Step 2: In the Hero section, click the gear icon to edit the section.
Step 3: Add a Fullwidth Header Module and select "Video Background" to upload an engaging video.
Step 4: Customize the text by editing the Header Content field.
Service Blocks:
Step 1: In Divi Builder, add a Blurb Module for each service.
Step 2: Use icons and short descriptions for each service (e.g., "Video Marketing" with a camera icon).
Step 3: Adjust the layout to a 3-column grid using Divi's section layout options.

2. Color Scheme
Change Button Colors:
Step 1: In the Divi Builder, click on a button module or create one by adding a Button Module.
Step 2: Go to the Design Tab > Button Settings > Button Color and select a bold, contrasting color (e.g., orange or blue).
Step 3: Save and preview changes.

3. Typography Adjustments
Increase Font Size & Use Bold Text:
Step 1: Edit any text module.
Step 2: In the Design Tab, adjust the Text Settings to increase the font size for headings (H1, H2, H3).
Step 3: Use the Bold button in the text editor for emphasis on key points.

4. Whitespace and Content Spacing
Step 1: In the Divi Builder, click on any row or section.
Step 2: Go to the Design Tab > Spacing > Margin and increase the margin size to create more whitespace.
Step 3: Adjust Padding under the same settings to balance text and images.

5. Visual Content Updates
Add Testimonial Videos & Logos:
Step 1: Add a Video Module by clicking the “+” icon, then upload testimonial videos from clients.
Step 2: To add client logos, use an Image Module. Upload logos and place them in a grid layout using multiple Image Modules side by side.
Add Before-and-After Sliders:
Step 1: Use Divi’s Before/After Slider Module to create a comparison slider. Upload your "before" and "after" images.
Step 2: Customize labels for the slider under Content Settings.

6. Mobile Optimization
Step 1: In the Divi Builder, click the Phone Icon at the bottom left to switch to mobile view.
Step 2: Adjust the Spacing, Padding, and Font Size under each module's Design Tab > Mobile Settings for better responsiveness.
Step 3: Use the Preview Feature to check both mobile and tablet views.

7. Visual Hierarchy Improvements
Prioritize CTA Placement:
Step 1: Ensure that key CTAs (e.g., "Get a Free Audit") are placed in the first section using a Button Module.
Step 2: Customize the Font Size and Background Color in the Design Tab of the button to ensure it stands out visually.

8. Add Hover Animations
Step 1: Edit any module (like buttons or images).
Step 2: Go to the Design Tab > Hover Effects and choose effects like Grow, Zoom, or Slide to make elements interactive.
Step 3: Test how each element reacts when hovered over by previewing the page.

By following these steps using Divi, you can significantly improve the visual appeal, usability, and functionality of your website. This will help engage visitors, improve SEO, and boost conversions!

Landing Page Structure

Step-by-Step Instructions to Build on WordPress (Using Divi)
1. Set Up the Landing Page:
In the WordPress dashboard, navigate to Pages > Add New.
Name the page (e.g., “Free Content Strategy Audit”).
Select Divi Builder to design the page layout.
2. Build the Header Section:
Add a Fullwidth Section with a Text Module for the headline.
Customize the text style, using bold colors for the title to grab attention.
3. Create Lead Magnet Download Section:
Add a new Row with two columns:
Left column: Add a Text Module describing the lead magnet.
Right column: Add an Image Module for the eBook cover (or visual representing the lead magnet).
Below the row, add a Button Module with the CTA "Download Now & Qualify for a Free Audit."
Link this button to your email opt-in system (e.g., Mailchimp) where users will receive the guide after entering their email.
4. Create the Qualifying Form Section:
Add a Form Module using Divi or an integrated plugin (like WPForms or Contact Form 7).
Set the form fields (Name, Email, Business Name, Marketing Budget, Challenges) based on the details above.
Style the form to be consistent with the rest of the site’s branding.
Add a Button Module labeled “Submit & Schedule My Free Audit.”
5. Add a Testimonial Section:
Add a new Row with two or three Testimonial Modules.
Insert real testimonials or video reviews from clients.
6. Include the Audit Overview Section:

Highlight key benefits and actionable insights that businesses will receive.
7. Add a Call Scheduling Section:

8. Optimize for Mobile:

Adjust font sizes, margins, and spacing for optimal mobile experience.
9. SEO Optimization:

Add meta descriptions and optimize for fast page loading (compress images and use caching).
By implementing this structure, your landing page will not only capture qualified leads but also guide them through a seamless process to schedule a free content strategy audit with Carr Multimedia.

Topics to include things like… why content strategy is important, why video is the best medium, how improvements can be made with higher conversion rates, higher revenue and profits, time saving, increasing productivity, why now is the time to invest in content strategy and marketing efforts, etc. topics to be spread out through different emails. Sure, I can help you create a 10-email marketing sequence for the content strategist targeting medium to large business owners in the Austin, Texas area. Here's a suggested outline for the sequence:

Highlight current market trends and the potential for growth in the region.

You said:
Write a 500 word email/video script explaining the benefits of content strategy in marketing efforts and how it benefits every kind of business
ChatGPT said:
ChatGPT
Subject: Unlocking the Power of Content Strategy: Transform Your Business Today!
Dear [Recipient's Name],
I hope this email finds you well. As a content strategist specializing in video marketing, I wanted to reach out and share some insights that could revolutionize your business's marketing efforts. In today's digital era, content strategy has emerged as the key to success, regardless of the size or nature of your business. With video as the primary medium, the opportunities for growth and success are boundless.
The Benefits of Content Strategy:
Establishing a Strong Online Presence: In a world where online visibility is paramount, content strategy empowers your business to be present and active across various digital platforms. Through targeted video content, you can reach your target audience with ease and create lasting impressions.
Building Authentic Connections: Video content allows you to humanize your brand, fostering deeper connections with your audience. Storytelling through video evokes emotions, making your business more relatable and trustworthy.
Enhancing Search Engine Visibility: Search engines prioritize video content, leading to higher rankings in search results. A well-optimized video strategy can significantly improve your website's SEO, attracting more organic traffic.
Boosting Engagement and Interaction: Videos have an inherent ability to captivate and engage viewers. By incorporating engaging video content, you can increase user interaction, encouraging shares and comments that amplify your brand's reach.
Driving Conversions and Sales: With a well-crafted content strategy, you can guide your potential customers seamlessly through the marketing funnel. Video testimonials, product demonstrations, and explainer videos can lead to higher conversion rates and increased sales.
Catering to Diverse Audiences: Every business serves a diverse clientele. Video content can adapt to cater to different audience segments, ensuring that your message resonates with each group effectively.
Demonstrating Thought Leadership: By sharing valuable insights and knowledge through video content, you position your business as an authority in your industry. Thought leadership fosters trust and credibility, attracting more clients and customers.
Content Strategy Benefits Every Business:
Whether you're a startup or an established enterprise, content strategy holds incredible potential for growth and success:
Small Businesses: Content strategy levels the playing field, allowing smaller businesses to compete with larger ones on a digital scale. Affordable and creative video content can amplify your brand's reach without breaking the bank.
Medium-Sized Enterprises: For businesses looking to expand their customer base and market share, a well-defined content strategy can open new doors and tap into previously untapped markets.
Large Corporations: Even established giants can't afford to overlook content strategy. Video marketing helps refresh and revitalize your brand, keeping it relevant in the ever-changing digital landscape.
B2B Companies: Content strategy helps build trust and foster relationships in the B2B space. Engaging video content can communicate complex solutions effectively, attracting more partnerships and collaborations.
E-commerce Ventures: Video content can significantly impact online sales, reducing the likelihood of returns and increasing customer satisfaction.
Incorporating a comprehensive content strategy centered around video will undoubtedly elevate your marketing efforts and drive remarkable results. It's time to embrace the power of video content and propel your business forward in the digital age.
I would love to discuss how our content strategy services can be tailored to meet the unique needs of your business. Feel free to schedule a complimentary consultation at your convenience via [insert contact information].
Let's embark on this transformative journey together, unlocking the full potential of your business through the art of video content strategy.
Warmest regards,
[Your Name] Content Strategist and Video Marketing Specialist

Legaisee-website copy
LEGAISEE
Business Archaeology & Authority Systems
Tagline: Excavate What Made You Great. Modernize It. Make It Work Again.

￼
HOME PAGE
Hero Section
Headline: Your Business Has a Buried Golden Era. We Know How to Find It.
Subheadline: Legaisee excavates the marketing assets, strategies, and positioning that made your company successful—then rebuilds them as modern authority systems that work in 2026.
Primary CTA: [Start Your Excavation — Free 15-Minute Snapshot]
Secondary CTA: [See How It Works]

￼
The Problem (Above the Fold)
Most businesses have forgotten what made them successful.
They abandoned working tactics before their time—chasing trends, copying competitors, or simply letting institutional knowledge walk out the door when key people left.
The result? Declining results, generic messaging, and the nagging sense that you used to be somebody in your market.
You're not wrong. You used to be. We can prove it.

￼
The Legaisee Method: Three Phases
Phase 1: Excavation
We conduct forensic research across 40+ channels—public archives, private collections, employee interviews, and digital archaeology—to reconstruct your marketing history with documentary precision.
Deliverable: The Business History Audit (10-page report + video walkthrough + asset portfolio)
Phase 2: Restoration
We modernize your proven assets for today's platforms, rebuild your voice using AI systems trained on your authentic communication style, and install the machinery to produce authority content at scale.
Deliverable: The Authority System (4 integrated workflows + team training)
Phase 3: Amplification
We manage ongoing execution, optimize based on performance data, and continuously excavate new competitive intelligence to keep your advantage sharp.
Deliverable: Monthly partnership (strategy, optimization, emergency support)

￼
Proof Before Promise: The Shallow Dig
[Free Lead Magnet Section]
Before you spend a dollar, see what we can find.
Submit your business name and website. In 15 minutes, our AI-powered excavation system will deliver a one-page Business Archaeology Snapshot showing:
 • Your most likely "golden era" and what worked then
 • One specific abandoned asset with modernization potential
 • The competitive gap that opened when you stopped
 • A specific question worth investigating further
[Get Your Free Snapshot]
No call required. No spam. Just intelligence.

￼
Who We Serve
Legaisee is for established businesses with:
 • 5+ years of operation (the deeper the history, the richer the excavation)
 • $500K+ annual revenue (proven business model, not startup experimentation)
 • Previous marketing investment (you've done real campaigns, not just "posting")
 • Decision-maker access (we work directly with owners, founders, and CEOs)
 • Openness to AI tools (we build systems, not one-off deliverables)
Industries where Business Archaeology delivers exceptional returns:
 • Local Services (HVAC, roofing, plumbing, electrical)
 • Professional Services (law, accounting, consulting, medical practices)
 • Manufacturing & B2B (industrial, distribution, trade specialists)
 • Retail & E-commerce (multi-generational, catalog heritage, brick-and-mortar legacy)
 • Healthcare & Wellness (practices with 10+ year histories)

￼
The Legaisee Difference
We don't guess. We excavate.
Most agencies start with "what should you do?" We start with "what made you great?" The difference is provenance vs. speculation.
We don't just deliver documents. We build systems.
You get working AI workflows, trained on your voice, that your team can operate. Not a strategy deck that sits in a drawer.
We specialize in what others ignore.
The past. Institutional memory. Abandoned tactics that worked. While others chase the new new thing, we find your durable advantage.

￼
Social Proof Preview
"I had no idea we used to run that campaign. When John showed me the 1998 newspaper ads and the customer response data, it clicked—we stopped being 'the neighborhood experts' and started being 'the cheap option' in 2004. We're fixing that now."
— [Name], [Title], [Company], [Industry]
[Read Full Case Studies]

￼
Final CTA
Your history is your competitive advantage. It's just buried.
Start with a free Shallow Dig. See what we find. Then decide if you want the full excavation.
[Get Your Free 15-Minute Snapshot]

￼
ABOUT PAGE
The Legaisee Story
Headline: Why Business Archaeology? Why Now?
The Origin
Legaisee was founded on a contradiction: businesses spend fortunes predicting the future while ignoring their own proven past.
We watched companies chase algorithm changes, platform fads, and competitor moves—while sitting on decades of market-tested positioning, messaging, and tactics that they'd simply... forgotten.
The metaphor of archaeology chose us. Like cultural archaeologists who reconstruct civilizations from fragments, we reconstruct business success from archives, memories, and digital traces.
The Name
Legaisee — from legare (to bind, to connect) and expertise, with the discernment of see. We bind your past to your future through expert excavation. We help you see what others miss.

￼
Our Methodology: The Legaisee Standard
Documentary Rigor
Every claim in our audits carries a confidence rating: [Verified], [Probable], [Plausible], [Uncertain]. We triangulate across multiple sources. We label uncertainty rather than hide it.
Source Hierarchy
 • Tier 1: Contemporaneous documents, financial records, third-party verification
 • Tier 2: Witness testimony, physical artifacts with provenance
 • Tier 3: Single-source memory claims
 • Tier 4: Hearsay (noted but not relied upon)
The Triangulation Protocol
When sources conflict—previous owners vs. employees, documents vs. memory—we don't pick winners. We document the conflict, assess credibility, and present the most probable synthesis with full transparency.

￼
The Team
[Your Name], Principal Archaeologist
[Your background—journalism, research, marketing, or relevant expertise]
[Your specific qualification: X years researching business history, Y industries excavated, Z assets recovered]
Philosophy: "The best marketing strategy isn't invented. It's remembered, modernized, and mechanized."

￼
Our Standards
We don't work with:
 • Startups without history (nothing to excavate)
 • Businesses seeking "viral" tactics (we build durable systems, not lottery tickets)
 • Companies where we can't access decision-makers (archaeology requires curator cooperation)
 • Organizations unwilling to document their systems (we build for permanence)
We guarantee:
 • Every audit finding will be sourced and rated for confidence
 • Every system will be trained on your authentic voice (not generic best practices)
 • Every deliverable will include team training (we don't leave you with tools you can't use)

￼
SERVICES PAGE
Service Architecture: The Three Stages

￼
Stage 1: The Business History Audit
Excavation & Documentation
Investment: $2,000 (Foundation) / $3,500 (Elevation) / $5,000-7,500 (Premium)
Duration: 5-7 business days
What We Do:
Conduct comprehensive forensic research across your business history—public records, private archives, employee interviews, and competitive intelligence—to reconstruct what made you successful, when you stopped doing it, and what it would take to restore that advantage.
The Excavation Process:
Days 1-2: Multi-Source Research
 • Public archaeology: Web archives, social media history, YouTube, news databases
 • Private artifact recovery: Hard drives, email archives, filing cabinets, employee interviews
 • Competitive mapping: Who took your space when you declined, what they did differently
Days 3-4: Synthesis & Analysis
 • Triangulate findings across sources
 • Identify golden era (peak performance period)
 • Document abandonment timeline and causes
 • Map modernization opportunities
Days 5-7: Documentation & Delivery
 • 10-page Business History Report
 • Asset portfolio (15-25 documented artifacts)
 • Video walkthrough (Loom presentation)
 • Timeline visualization (key campaigns, transitions, competitive shifts)
Deliverables:
 • Written report with confidence-rated findings
 • Digital asset museum (organized, tagged, searchable)
 • Competitive gap analysis
 • Modernization recommendations
 • 60-minute presentation call
[Schedule a Discovery Call]

￼
Stage 2: The Authority System Build
Restoration & Mechanization
Investment: $5,500 (Foundation) / $8,500 (Elevation) / $12,000-18,000 (Premium)
Duration: 21 days (3 weeks)
What We Do:
Transform your excavated assets into modern, AI-powered content systems that produce authority-building material in your authentic voice—at scale, without your constant involvement.
The Four Core Systems:
System 1: Content Production Engine
 • Script generator (trained on your voice profile)
 • Hook tester (rates and improves opening lines)
 • Repurposing engine (one piece → platform ecosystem)
 • Title/thumbnail optimizer (CTR-focused)
System 2: Publishing Rhythm
 • Editorial calendar architecture
 • Scheduling tool integration
 • Approval workflow design
 • Batch production training (4 hours = 2 weeks of content)
System 3: Performance Feedback Loop
 • Metrics dashboard (awareness, engagement, conversion)
 • Weekly review ritual (30-minute decision framework)
 • Optimization triggers (when to pivot vs. persist)
 • Monthly deep-dive protocol
System 4: Asset Library & Reuse
 • Organized content archive (find anything in 60 seconds)
 • Version control protocols
 • Refresh workflows (update evergreen content)
 • Remix systems (adapt top performers across platforms)
Training Included:
 • Day 17: AI Workflows Training (4 hours)
 • Day 18: Publishing & Performance Training (4 hours)
 • Day 19: Soft Launch (produce first week together)
 • Day 20: Documentation & Video Library
 • Day 21: Handoff & Retainer Transition
Deliverables:
 • 4 working AI systems (prompts, workflows, tested)
 • Client Operations Manual (complete documentation)
 • Video training library (5 Loom walkthroughs)
 • Team capable of independent operation
 • 30-day support buffer
[Inquire About Stage 2]

￼
Stage 3: The Amplification Partnership
Ongoing Optimization & Intelligence
Investment: $2,750/month (Foundation) / $4,500/month (Elevation) / $6,000-10,000/month (Premium)
Duration: Ongoing (month-to-month, 90-day minimum)
What We Do:
Maintain your authority system, optimize based on performance data, conduct ongoing competitive intelligence, and provide strategic partnership for continuous advantage.
Monthly Deliverables:
Weekly:
 • Content review & async video feedback (Loom)
 • Optimization recommendations
 • Trend alerts (industry shifts, platform changes)
Monthly:
 • 60-minute strategy call
 • Performance report (metrics, analysis, adjustments)
 • Content calendar refinement
 • System updates (prompt improvements, new templates)
Quarterly:
 • 2-hour strategy deep-dive
 • Competitive archaeology update (what's changed in your market)
 • Asset refresh (update evergreen content)
 • Strategy pivot recommendations (if indicated)
As Needed:
 • 48-hour response to emergencies
 • Crisis protocol activation (if results decline)
 • Additional training for new team members
Scope:
 • 8 hours/month direct service (Foundation/Elevation)
 • 12 hours/month direct service (Premium)
 • Unlimited async communication
 • Priority scheduling
[Apply for Stage 3 Partnership]

￼
Stage 4: On-Camera Authority Coaching
Personal Presence & Platform Mastery
Investment: $3,750 (Foundation) / $5,500 (Elevation) / $7,500-12,000 (Premium)
Duration: 16 hours (modular, typically 4 weeks)
What We Do:
Transform you from "business owner who posts" to "recognized authority in your field" through on-camera presence training, platform-specific skills, and personal brand architecture.
Modules:
Module 1: On-Camera Foundations
 • Posture, framing, lighting setup
 • Teleprompter vs. natural delivery
 • Energy calibration (authentic, not performative)
Module 2: Delivery Naturalization

 • Conversational tone development
 • Handling mistakes with grace
Module 3: Technical Excellence

 • Background design (intentional, not accidental)
 • Multi-camera and B-roll integration
Module 4: Platform-Specific Skills
 • TikTok: Pattern interrupts, trending audio, comment strategy

 • LinkedIn: Professional authority, document posts, newsletter strategy

Module 5: Crisis Management
 • Handling mistakes on camera

￼
THE SHALLOW DIG PAGE
(Dedicated Landing Page for Lead Magnet)
Hero
Headline: Discover Your Business's Buried Marketing Gold

No call required. No credit card. Just intelligence.

￼
How It Works
Step 1: Submit

METHODOLOGY PAGE
The Legaisee Method: Documentary Rigor Applied to Business
Introduction:
Business Archaeology applies the standards of historical research to marketing strategy. We don't speculate. We excavate, triangulate, and verify.

￼
The 8-Dimension Voice Profile
Before we write a word of content, we capture your authentic communication style across eight dimensions:
1. Vocabulary Register — Technical vs. accessible, jargon density, formality
2. Sentence Architecture — Length, complexity, rhythm, fragment usage
3. Humor & Tone — Type, frequency, enthusiasm, emotional openness
4. Perspective & Stance — Expert vs. peer, teacher vs. learner, optimist vs. realist
5. Transitions & Flow — Connector preferences, storytelling signals, pacing
6. CTAs & Directives — Command strength, urgency, benefit framing
7. Evidence & Proof — Data usage, anecdote style, authority signaling
8. Visual Language — Metaphor frequency, sensory detail, scene-setting
Result: AI systems that write in your voice, not generic "marketing voice."

￼
Source Quality Hierarchy
Tier 1 (Verified):
 • Contemporaneous documents with metadata
 • Financial records (budgets, invoices, actual spend)
 • Third-party verification (industry publications, awards, news coverage)
 • Multiple independent witnesses with matching stories
Tier 2 (Probable):
 • Single witness with specific details (dates, names, amounts)
 • Physical artifacts with provenance
 • Partial documents corroborated by other sources
Tier 3 (Plausible):
 • Single witness with vague recollections
 • Memory without documentation
 • Self-interested testimony
Tier 4 (Uncertain):
 • Hearsay
 • Suspected falsehoods
 • Agenda-driven claims without corroboration
Every finding in our reports carries a confidence label. We don't hide uncertainty—we document it.

￼
The Triangulation Protocol
When sources conflict (and they do), we:
1. Document the conflict — All versions recorded
2. Assess credibility — Tier rating, bias analysis, motive examination
3. Seek resolution — "How could both be partially true?"
4. Synthesize transparently — "Source A claims X, Source B claims Y. The most probable synthesis is Z, with [confidence level]."
Example:
"Previous owner denies TV advertising; two employees confirm 'Game Day Special' spots; budget document shows $60K 'Broadcast' line item 2000. Conclusion: Significant TV presence 2000-2001, likely discontinued 2002. Previous owner may confuse with later period. [Probable]"

￼
The 3-Source Rule
No significant claim appears in our reports without:
 • Minimum 2 independent sources, OR
 • 1 Tier 1 source alone
This is non-negotiable. We'd rather report "uncertain" than be wrong.

￼
The Private Archaeology Protocol
Public sources find the surface. We go deeper:
Digital Artifacts: Old hard drives, email archives, CRM exports, cloud accounts, design files, video projects
Physical Artifacts: Filing cabinets, print samples, direct mail, trade show materials, awards, correspondence
Human Artifacts: Previous owners, longtime employees, customers, vendors, industry historians
Access Negotiation: We tailor our approach to positive, neutral, or negative relationships with gatekeepers. We respect refusal. We triangulate around gaps.

￼
Continuous Verification
Our reports are living documents. New information emerges:
 • Addendums: Minor corrections or additions (free)
 • Revised Reports: Major new findings (50% of audit price)
 • Dispute Resolution: If sources challenge findings, we investigate and correct if warranted
We never defend errors. We correct them.

￼

ABOUT BUSINESS ARCHAEOLOGY PAGE
(Educational/Thought Leadership)
What Is Business Archaeology?
Business Archaeology is the systematic excavation, documentation, and modernization of a company's historical marketing assets, strategies, and positioning.
Like cultural archaeology reconstructs ancient civilizations from fragments, Business Archaeology reconstructs business success from archives, memories, and digital traces.

￼
Why It Works
The Lindy Effect: Things that have lasted tend to last longer. Marketing tactics that worked for 10 years have proven durability. Tactics invented last month have not.
Pattern Recognition: Your business has succeeded before. That success leaves traces. We find the patterns and rebuild them.
Institutional Memory Loss: When key people leave, knowledge walks out the door. We recover it before it's gone forever.
Competitive Asymmetry: While competitors chase trends, you own provenance. Trends expire. Provenance compounds.

￼
The 40+ Channel Taxonomy
We research across eight major classes, 40+ specific channels:
Print (1890s-present): Newspapers, magazines, direct mail, outdoor, point-of-sale, directories, premiums
Broadcast Radio (1920s-present): Network, local, satellite, podcast, streaming
Broadcast Television (1940s-present): Network, local, cable, satellite, public
Cinema/Video (1900s-present): Theaters, in-flight, closed circuit, place-based, physical media
Digital Web (1990s-present): Display, search, email, websites, forums, blogs
Mobile (2000s-present): SMS, in-app, mobile web, push notifications, location-based
Social Media (2000s-present): Meta, video platforms, professional networks, microblogging, visual platforms, ephemeral content, audio, emerging
Advanced Digital (2010s-present): CTV/OTT, programmatic, audio streaming, gaming, DOOH, connected home, VR/AR, AI/conversational

￼
When Business Archaeology Delivers Exceptional ROI
 • Pre-2010 marketing investment: You did real campaigns, not just "posting"
 • Founder/previous owner transition: Knowledge loss occurred
 • Family/multi-generational business: Deep history, forgotten wisdom
 • Market position decline: You used to dominate, now you don't
 • Commoditization pressure: Competing on price when you used to compete on value
 • "We used to be somebody": The feeling that your best days are behind you

￼

ADDITIONAL PAGE: FAQ
Frequently Asked Questions
How is Business Archaeology different from market research?
Market research tells you what customers want now. Business Archaeology tells you what made customers choose you then—and how to make them choose you again. We study your history, not just your market.
What if we don't have archives or old files?
We triangulate from multiple sources. Employee interviews, customer memories, public records, competitor observations—we build the picture from whatever fragments exist. Some of our richest excavations came from businesses that thought they had "nothing."
How long does it take to see results?
Stage 1 (Audit) delivers clarity in 5-7 days. Stage 2 (Systems) takes 21 days to build, then 30-90 days to see metric movement. Stage 3 (Partnership) optimizes continuously. Most clients see engagement improvements in 30 days, lead improvements in 60-90 days.
Do we have to use AI?
Our systems use AI to scale your voice, not replace it. You approve everything. The AI generates drafts; you refine. Over time, the AI learns, and your effort decreases. But you're always in control.
What if we don't like what you find?
Sometimes archaeology reveals uncomfortable truths (bad decisions, missed opportunities). We deliver findings with context and forward-looking recommendations. The past informs; it doesn't condemn.
Can you work with our existing marketing team?
Yes. We often enhance rather than replace. Your team gets systems that make them more effective, not directives that ignore their expertise.
What industries don't fit Business Archaeology?
Startups without history, businesses that never invested in marketing, companies seeking "viral" shortcuts, or organizations where we can't access decision-makers.
How do you handle confidential information?
NDAs standard. Source confidentiality respected (we anonymize when requested). Private documents returned or destroyed per client preference. We treat your history with the discretion of archivists.

Your Business Has a Buried Golden Era.
 We Know Where to Dig.
There was a time when your company moved differently.
Decisions landed cleaner. Customers responded faster. Your name meant something specific in the market—something competitors couldn’t replicate, no matter how closely they watched.
That wasn’t luck. And it wasn’t temporary.
It was built on positioning, messaging, and strategic moves that worked—repeatedly, predictably—until, at some point, they stopped being used.
Not because they failed.
Because they were forgotten.

The Recognition
Most businesses don’t decline all at once.
They drift.
A campaign gets replaced. A message gets softened. A strategy gets abandoned—not because it stopped working, but because something newer looked more promising at the time.
Then someone leaves. Then another. Files disappear. Decisions lose context. What used to be deliberate becomes reactive.
And slowly, almost invisibly, the company that once had a clear position in the market becomes interchangeable with everyone else trying to compete in it.
If you’ve ever had the sense that your business used to operate at a higher level—more respected, more effective, more certain—you’re not imagining it.
That version existed.
The problem is no one documented it well enough to preserve it.
Until now.

The Method
We don’t begin with assumptions about what you should do next.
We begin with evidence of what already worked.
Every engagement starts the same way: by reconstructing your company’s history with the discipline of an archivist and the skepticism of an investigator.
We trace campaigns across time. We locate fragments others overlook—old advertisements, internal documents, customer responses, overlooked assets, conversations that were never meant to be preserved but left patterns behind anyway.
We cross-reference sources. We resolve contradictions. We establish what is verified, what is probable, and what is simply noise.
What emerges is not a theory.
It is a documented record of how your business actually won in the market—when it did, why it worked, and precisely where that advantage was lost.
From there, the work changes.
What was recovered is restored.
What was restored is modernized.
What is modernized is installed as a working system—one your team can operate without guesswork, without dependency, and without losing it again.
This is not reinvention.
It is controlled reactivation.

Proof Before Permission
Before any engagement, you are given a small piece of the process.
A surface-level excavation. Fast, precise, and revealing enough to determine whether deeper work is justified.
You provide your business name and digital presence.
Within minutes, the system scans, correlates, and reconstructs a preliminary view of your historical positioning—identifying a likely peak period, an overlooked asset with restoration potential, and a gap your competitors may have filled when that asset disappeared.
No call is required. No commitment is expected.
Just a single document—enough to answer one question:
Is there something here worth recovering?
If there is, you’ll see it immediately.
If there isn’t, you’ll know just as quickly.
Request your Snapshot.

Who This Is For
This work is not for companies still searching for their first success.
It is for businesses that have already built something real.
Organizations with history. With movement behind them. With decisions that mattered, even if the reasons for those decisions have faded over time.
Companies that have invested in marketing before—seriously, not casually—and understand the difference between activity and strategy.
Operators who recognize that what they’re missing is not effort, but clarity.
And who are willing to examine their own past with enough honesty to recover what was left behind.

The Difference
Most firms begin with the future.
Predictions. Trends. Hypotheses about what might work next.
We begin with the past.
Not as nostalgia, but as evidence.
Because a strategy that already worked under real conditions—against real competitors, with real customers—carries a kind of weight that speculation never will.
We don’t deliver ideas.
We deliver provenance.
We don’t hand over recommendations.
We install systems.
And we don’t rely on memory.
We document everything—so what is recovered now cannot be lost again later.

A Familiar Moment
There is a point in nearly every excavation where the same thing happens.
A document surfaces. An old campaign. A positioning statement no one has used in years.
Someone pauses.
Reads it again.
And realizes, usually in a single moment, exactly when things changed.
Not dramatically. Not all at once.
Just a small shift that, over time, became a completely different business.
That moment is where the work becomes valuable.
Because once you can see it clearly—
You can rebuild it intentionally.

Closing
Your competitive advantage isn’t missing.
It isn’t outdated.
And it isn’t something you need to invent from scratch.
It’s already yours.
It’s just buried under years of decisions that were never meant to erase it—but did.
We find it.
We restore it.
And we make sure it works again.

Why This Work Exists
Most businesses don’t lose their edge because they run out of ideas.
They lose it because they abandon the ones that already worked.
Not all at once. Not intentionally.
A campaign gets replaced by something more modern. A message gets softened to appeal to a broader audience. A strategy that once required discipline gets traded for something easier to execute.
At the time, each decision makes sense.
But over years, those decisions accumulate. The sharpness dulls. The positioning drifts. What was once distinct becomes difficult to describe.
And eventually, the company no longer knows what made it effective in the first place.
This isn’t a failure of intelligence.
It’s a failure of preservation.
Because most businesses were never built to remember themselves.

The Realization
Legaisee was not built on a new idea.
It was built on a pattern.
The same pattern appearing across industries, across markets, across companies that had once been dominant in their space.
When examined closely, the story was almost always the same.
There had been a period—sometimes years, sometimes decades—where everything worked.
Marketing produced consistent results. Customers responded predictably. Growth followed a rhythm that didn’t require constant reinvention.
Then something changed.
Not because the market disappeared.
But because the company did.
Not physically.
Strategically.
The original positioning faded. The messaging shifted. The underlying advantage—whatever made the business different—was slowly replaced with something more generic, more reactive, more aligned with what everyone else was doing at the time.
And once that happened, performance didn’t collapse.
It declined gradually.
Quietly enough that no single decision could be blamed.
Loudly enough that something felt off.

The Approach
Most responses to that decline focus forward.
New strategies. New platforms. New tactics designed to “fix” the problem.
But that assumes the problem is a lack of innovation.
In most cases, it isn’t.
It’s a loss of continuity.
Legaisee approaches the problem from the opposite direction.
Instead of asking what should be created next, we ask what already existed—and why it worked.
We reconstruct the business as it actually operated during its strongest period.
Not from memory alone, but from evidence.
Documents. Campaigns. Internal materials. External responses. Patterns that persist across sources even when individual accounts conflict.
We establish what is verifiable. We separate signal from revisionist memory. We document the conditions that allowed that success to exist in the first place.
Only after that reconstruction is complete do we move forward.
Because once the original advantage is understood clearly, the path forward is no longer speculative.
It becomes structural.

The Standard
This work follows a discipline most marketing has never adopted.
Every meaningful claim is treated as something that must be supported.
Not assumed. Not inferred. Supported.
Sources are evaluated. Conflicts are documented. Uncertainty is labeled rather than hidden.
If two versions of a story exist, both are examined.
If documentation contradicts memory, the contradiction is preserved and resolved—not ignored.
What emerges is not a narrative designed to persuade.
It is a record designed to hold up under scrutiny.
That distinction matters.
Because decisions built on assumption tend to fail the moment conditions change.
Decisions built on verified patterns tend to hold.

What Gets Recovered
What most companies lose is not just information.
It’s structure.
The way messaging was constructed. The way offers were framed. The way customers moved through decisions without friction.
These are not always written down.
But they leave traces.
In language. In repetition. In results that appear consistently over time even when the surface execution changes.
Legaisee identifies those patterns, isolates them, and reconstructs them in a form that can be used again.
Not as historical reference.
As operational systems.

Why It Matters Now
There was a time when losing institutional knowledge was inconvenient.
Now it’s expensive.
Markets move faster. Teams turn over more frequently. Decisions are made under pressure, often without the context that originally justified them.
At the same time, businesses are being pushed toward constant reinvention—new platforms, new formats, new strategies—without ever resolving what actually worked before.
The result is a cycle of effort without accumulation.
Work gets done.
But nothing compounds.
Business Archaeology exists to break that cycle.
By restoring continuity.
By reconnecting present decisions to proven structures.
By ensuring that what worked once is not lost again simply because it wasn’t documented properly the first time.

The Principle
The strongest strategies are rarely invented.
They are uncovered.
Refined.
And reintroduced under conditions where they can operate at a higher level than before.
This is not about returning to the past.
It is about using the past with precision.
Because when a business understands exactly what made it effective—
It stops guessing.
And starts operating with intent again.

Closing
Most companies are sitting on a version of themselves that was more effective than the one they’re running today.
Not because the market was easier.
But because the strategy was clearer.
That version still exists.
Not intact.
But recoverable.
Legaisee is built to find it.
And make sure it doesn’t get lost again.
