Service Tier
Tier 1 or Tier 2


Save sheet in your shared folder for future assistants.


Tip: This sheet acts as your free CRM to track all leads and clients.

Step 2 — Identify Potential Clients
Step-by-Step:
Open Google Maps or Yelp.


Search for local businesses in your niche (restaurants, retail, professional services).


Check if they have social media accounts with low followers.


Copy business info into CRM sheet:


Client Name


Contact Name (owner/manager if available)


Email


Social Links


Tip: Low followers = high potential for your strategy services.

Step 3 — Research the Business
Step-by-Step:
Open the business’s social media accounts.


Check:


How many followers


Frequency of posts


Engagement (likes/comments)


Record strengths, weaknesses, and opportunities in the “Notes” column of CRM.


Tip: This research will help you personalize your pitch.

Step 4 — Outreach (Cold Contact)
Step-by-Step:
Draft an email or message. Use template in your Doc:


Example Email Template:
Subject: Helping [Business Name] grow social media presence  

Hi [Contact Name],  

I noticed [Business Name] has a social media presence but could reach more customers. I help small businesses like yours grow social media engagement with simple, actionable content strategies.  

I’d love to offer a free 15-minute consultation to show how you could attract more clients online.  

Would you be available for a call this week?  

Best,  
John Carr  
Carr Multimedia

Send via Email or LinkedIn DM.


Update Status in CRM → “Contacted”.


Set Follow-Up Date (e.g., 3 days later if no reply).



Step 5 — Pitch & Consultation
Step-by-Step:
Schedule a 15–30 min call (Zoom or phone).


During the call:


Ask about their business goals


Show examples of your Tier 1 / Tier 2 services


Explain benefits clearly, referencing UVP & Vision from Module 1


Record notes in CRM → adjust “Notes” column for client-specific info.


After call, send proposal email including:


Recommended Tier


Price


Next steps



Step 6 — Onboarding New Client
Step-by-Step:
Update Status → “Onboarded”.


Send client:


Contract (simple Google Doc template)


Invoice for deposit or full payment (Google Docs/Sheets or free invoicing tool like Wave)


Create client folder in Google Drive:

 Carr Multimedia → Clients → [Client Name]


Add:


Research notes


Strategy documents


AI-generated content prompts


Templates


Tip: This ensures all client info is in one place for you and future assistants.

Step 7 — Strategy Execution & Follow-Up
Step-by-Step:
Create content plan / templates based on Tier purchased.


Share with client via Google Docs or Notion.


Schedule check-ins (weekly or bi-weekly depending on package).


Update CRM sheet with:


Tasks completed


Client feedback


Next follow-up date



Step 8 — Billing & Payment Tracking
Step-by-Step:
Keep a column in CRM: Paid / Unpaid


Send invoices for:


Deposit upfront (recommended)


Final payment after deliverables


Track received payments → update CRM sheet


Include notes for bookkeeping: software subscriptions, AI tool usage, business expenses



Step 9 — Optional Notes for Future Assistants
Save email/DM templates in shared folder.


Save AI prompt templates for client strategy.


Train assistants to update CRM sheet every time a task is completed.


Keep a checklist of client workflow steps for consistency.

Module 3 — Client Acquisition & Workflow (Expanded for Beginners)

Overview
This module teaches how to find, qualify, contact, onboard, deliver, and get paid by clients. It’s designed for someone with no prior experience in marketing or client management. Every step is written so a new employee or digital assistant could follow it.

1. Prospecting & Lead Generation (Finding Clients)
Objective: Identify businesses that can benefit from AI-driven content strategy but currently have underperforming social media.
Step-by-Step:
Define Your Target Niche


Example niches: Mexican restaurants, Italian restaurants, real estate agents, retail shops, small professional services (lawyers, accountants).


Keep a list of 3–5 niches to start.


Social Media Search


Go to Instagram / Facebook / LinkedIn / TikTok.


Search businesses in your target niche using hashtags or location filters (e.g., #GalvestonRestaurant, “Mexican restaurant Houston”).


Look for accounts with:


Less than 500 followers


Irregular posting (less than 3 posts/week)


Low engagement (<5 likes/comments per post)


Google & Yelp Search


Search “[niche] near [city]”


Check the first 10–20 results for:


Website exists


Social media links visible


Current marketing seems minimal


Networking & Referrals


Ask any contacts or friends for leads


Check local Chamber of Commerce member lists


Attend local business events or virtual networking


Document Leads in a Sheet


Columns to include:


Business Name


Contact Name & Role


Email / Phone


Social Media Links


Notes on gaps / opportunities


Priority (High / Medium / Low)


Use Google Sheets or Notion database



2. Lead Qualification (Selecting the Best Prospects)
Step-by-Step:
Check if business has existing social channels. If not, skip for now.


Look at posting frequency and engagement. Rate:


High: Good strategy already, may not need services


Medium: Could benefit from strategy


Low: Prime prospect


Estimate monthly marketing budget (if possible, based on website/ads or business type).


Flag priority leads in your sheet for first outreach.



3. Cold Outreach Workflow
Step-by-Step for Beginners:
Prepare Your Template Email / DM


Use ChatGPT to draft:


Friendly, professional tone


Reference the business by name


Mention a specific gap in their current content


Offer a free sample or consultation


Send Initial Email / DM


Example: “Hi [Name], I noticed your [Instagram/Facebook] and wanted to share some ideas on how you could grow your audience and engagement…”


Schedule Follow-Up


Wait 3–5 days


Send a short follow-up, include 1 AI-generated content idea snippet


Book Intro Call


Use Google Calendar / Calendly


Confirm date/time via email


Prepare a script/template for the call:


Introduce yourself


Ask about their business goals


Explain your Tier 1/Tier 2 offerings


Offer a sample insight or mini audit


Document Every Step in Your Sheet


Status column: Contacted / Followed Up / Meeting Booked / Not Interested



4. Client Onboarding (Once They Say Yes)
Step-by-Step:
Send Welcome Email


Include:


Contract / Terms (Google Doc or DocuSign)


First invoice (Stripe, PayPal, or bank transfer)


Send Client Info Form (Google Form)


Ask for:


Business name & website


Social media links


Branding guide / logos


Target audience & goals


Past content library / marketing materials


Set Up Client in CRM (Google Sheets / Notion)


Columns to include:


Client Name


Package Type (Tier 1 / Tier 2)


Onboarding Date


Payment Status


Deliverables Due Dates


AI Prompts Used


Notes / Feedback



5. Content Strategy Workflow
Step-by-Step:
Research & Audit


Review client social media and website


Check competitors’ content


Document observations


AI-Assisted Content Creation


Generate:


Content pillars


Scripts / captions


Posting schedule / calendar


Hashtags / SEO keywords


Client Review


Share draft strategy & calendar via Google Docs or Notion


Collect client feedback


Make adjustments if needed


Deliver Final Package


Tier 1: PDF or Notion link with strategy + calendar + AI prompts


Tier 2: Ongoing updates, coaching calls, and weekly check-ins



6. Payment & Admin Workflow
Step-by-Step:
Send Invoice


Use Google Docs / Sheets template or Stripe / PayPal


Include clear payment terms


Track Payment in CRM


Columns: Paid / Pending / Overdue


Schedule reminder emails if needed


Allocate ~30 min/month per client for admin


Billing, follow-up, reporting

Examples:

Module 4 — Website & CRM Integration (Beginner-Level Step-by-Step)

Home


Services

Click Install Now, then Activate.

Home


Services


About


Contact


Optional: Client Portal (password protected)

Services: Tier 1 & Tier 2 offerings, pricing table, CTA button.


Contact: Form for leads.



Step 4 — Create New Pages
Go to Pages → Add New.

Name


Email


Business Name


Website


Social Media Links


Short message / Goals

Step 6 — Connect Form to Google Sheets (Free CRM)

Publish page.

Module 5 — Marketing & Growth (Beginner-Level Step-by-Step)

Overview
This module teaches how to advertise Carr Multimedia, attract clients, and grow your business. Steps are beginner-friendly and can be executed with no budget, using free tools and simple processes.
By the end of this module, you will have:
A repeatable marketing workflow


Templates for outreach and social media


A system to track results and refine marketing



Step 1 — Create a Marketing Tracker
Tools Needed: Google Sheets (free)
Open Google Sheets → New Sheet → name it:

 Carr Multimedia Marketing Tracker


Add columns:


Column
Purpose
Date
When activity is done
Channel
Email, LinkedIn, Instagram, Referral, etc.
Action
Post, Email, DM, Call
Target Audience
Restaurant, Retail, etc.
Leads Generated
Number of potential clients contacted
Outcome
Response, Scheduled Call, No Response
Notes
Lessons learned / adjustments

Tip: This allows tracking which marketing activities actually produce leads.

Step 2 — Build Social Proof (Website & Free Platforms)
Step-by-Step:
On your Home & About pages, include:


Short bio highlighting 30+ years production experience


Free examples of past work (images, AI content templates)


Client testimonials (if any, or placeholders to add later)


Create LinkedIn / Instagram / Facebook / TikTok accounts (free):


Use consistent branding: logo, colors, fonts


Post 1–2 times per week initially: tips, AI-generated examples, small case studies



Step 3 — Identify Target Audience
Open your Google Sheet CRM from Module 3


Sort businesses by niche and social media presence


Focus on:


Restaurants, retail, professional services


Low follower count or low engagement


Record any new businesses discovered during free research in Marketing Tracker.


Tip: This ensures marketing is targeted and efficient.

Step 4 — Outreach Workflow
Step-by-Step:
Open Marketing Tracker


For each business, send outreach (email/DM/LinkedIn):


Use template from Module 3 (cold email or message)


Log date, channel, and action in tracker


Update outcome as responses come in


Template Reminder:
Hi [Name],  

I noticed your [social media platform] has potential for growth. I help small businesses like yours grow engagement and attract clients with simple content strategies.  

Would you like a free 15-minute consultation this week?  

Best,  
John Carr  
Carr Multimedia


Step 5 — Content Marketing for Lead Generation
Plan weekly posts:


Tips on social media growth


Mini case studies of small businesses (even fictional examples at first)


Demonstrate AI content creation


Schedule posts using free tools like:


Meta Business Suite (for Instagram & Facebook)


Later.com free plan (limited posts per month)


Track engagement and leads generated in Marketing Tracker



Step 6 — Networking & Referrals
Reach out to friends, past clients, or local business groups


Offer free advice or workshops for exposure


Track any leads generated in Marketing Tracker


Tip: Word-of-mouth is extremely effective for small business services.

Step 7 — Track Marketing Performance
Weekly review of Marketing Tracker


Identify:


Most successful channels


Types of messages that get responses


Content that gets the most engagement


Adjust marketing activities accordingly



Step 8 — Automate Where Possible
Use Make.com (free tier) or Zapier to:


Move new form submissions from website → CRM → send welcome email


Schedule social media posts automatically


Document workflows in Google Docs for assistants to follow


Tip: Automation saves time as the business grows.

Step 9 — Optional Paid Growth Later
Once revenue allows, consider:


Meta/Facebook Ads


Google Ads


AI tools to create social posts faster


Update Marketing Tracker to include paid campaigns


Monitor ROI before scaling

You can track marketing effectiveness in a sheet for future optimization.


Future assistants can execute marketing tasks, post content, and log results without supervision.
